# Neural Spectral Capacity with Residual Context for Heterogeneous Resource Allocation within Transformers

Chenyu Zhu , Ruoyu Zhao and Zhichao Lu

De<sub>p</sub>artment of Com<sub>p</sub>uter Science<sub>,</sub> Cit<sub>y</sub> Universit<sub>y</sub> of Hon<sub>g</sub> Kon<sub>g</sub>

Predicting relative trained performance from architectural specifications can guide design when training costs limit experimentation. Neural Spectral Capacity (NSC) provides such a heuristic, but scores projections independently and assigns identical scores to compatible reorderings of the same layer configurations. We introduce Residual-Aware Neural Spectral Capacity (ResNSC) for Transformers, using a specification-derived statistical reference for accumulated residual updates to determine the input and residual-state statistics in each projection’s capacity calculation, while retaining additive evaluation without data or instantiated weights. When residual widths vary, earlier choices afect later scores, so we augment NSC’s allocator (NSC-DP) with residual history beyond its layer-and-budget state. ResNSC-DP jointly allocates residual and feed-forward widths under a resource budget, with certified bounds on the best numerically evaluated ResNSC score within a specified finite candidate set. At the 121M and 374M pretraining scales, selected architectures achieve lower mean perplexity than uniform, variable-width, and tapered allocations on FineWeb-Edu, WikiText-2, and LAMBADA at comparable parameter budgets. In inherited-weight LLaMA-7B pruning using the LoNAS supernet, ResNSC-DP improves eight-task average accuracy over NSC by 3.28–5.46 percentage points across eight matched parameter budgets. Paired reversals preserve the projection shapes and NSC scores yet worsen performance in both settings, exposing performance diferences hidden by position-independent scoring. The code for this work is available at https://github.com/Optima-CityU/neural-spectral-capacity.

## 2. Related Work

Training-free architecture evaluation. Training-free architecture evaluation estimates candidate <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng eac</sub>h <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>.</sub> R<sub>epresen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s use ac</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y or gra</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t statistics at initialization (Abdelfattah et al., 2021; Mellor et al., 2021); W-PCA combines <sub>p</sub>arameter information with <sub>p</sub>rinci<sub>p</sub>al-com<sub>p</sub>onent statistics of intermediate activations (Wan<sub>g</sub>, 2025). These <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> NSC <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>es a</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c score</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> projection dimensions and prescribed initialization statistics, evaluating each projection independently of its <sub>p</sub>osition (Zhu et al., 2026). ResNSC retains this s<sub>p</sub>ecification-onl<sub>y</sub> evaluation while conditionin<sub>g</sub> projection-level scores on residual context.

Residual context in Transformers. The Transformer Circuits framework describes Transformer com<sub>p</sub>utation throu<sub>g</sub>h branches that read and u<sub>p</sub>date a shared residual re<sub>p</sub>resentation (Elha<sub>g</sub>e et al., 2021). The behavior of this shared state de<sub>p</sub>ends on normalization, studied throu<sub>g</sub>h si<sub>g</sub>nal and <sub>g</sub>radient <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation (Xion<sub>g</sub> et al., 2020), and residual mixin<sub>g</sub>, which H<sub>yp</sub>er-Connections modifies throu<sub>g</sub>h learned connections (Zhu et al., 2025). ResNSC uses this structural <sub>p</sub>ers<sub>p</sub>ective to construct a specification-derived reference for evaluating projections. Its statistical assumptions are modeling <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ces ma</sub>d<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure scor</sub>i<sub>ng, spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 3<sub>.</sub>2<sub>.</sub>

Layer-wise heterogeneous Transformer architectures. Architecture search explores layer-specific Transformer confi<sub>g</sub>urations, as in AutoFormer (Chen et al., 2021) and FlexiBERT (Tuli et al., 2022). VWT and TLM <sub>p</sub>rescribe de<sub>p</sub>th-de<sub>p</sub>endent <sub>p</sub>rofiles for whole-block and FFN widths, res<sub>p</sub>ectivel<sub>y</sub> (Ba<sub>y</sub>at et al., 2026; Wu et al., 2026), while <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>-<sub>g</sub>uided allocation uses forward-<sub>p</sub>ass measurements of re<sub>p</sub>resentation chan<sub>g</sub>e (Mudarisov et al., 2026). Structured <sub>p</sub>runin<sub>g</sub> also makes model-de<sub>p</sub>endent choices, usin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>radient-based im<sub>p</sub>ortance in LLM-Pruner (Ma et al., 2023) or learnin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>runin<sub>g</sub> confi<sub>g</sub>urations in Sheared LLaMA (Xia et al., 2024). ResNSC su<sub>pp</sub>lies a s<sub>p</sub>ecification-derived objective f<sub>or w</sub>idth <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>h<sub>er</sub>it<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>su</sub>b<sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> I<sub>n</sub> L<sub>o</sub>NAS (Munoz et al., 2024), ResNSC selects FFN widths while the elastic su<sub>p</sub>ernet su<sub>pp</sub>lies the selected <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>’<sub>s we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s.</sub> Wh<sub>en res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>w</sub>idth<sub>s vary, ear</sub>li<sub>er c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ces a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>er scores, so</sub> R<sub>es</sub>NSC<sub>-</sub>DP <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> NSC’<sub>s</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>-cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>a</sub>ll<sub>oca</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ith <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory.</sub>

## 3.1. Preliminaries: local capacity and NSC

Local capacity. Consider a linear transformation with weight matrix $W \in \mathbb { R } ^ { m \times n }$ <sub>.</sub> C<sub>on</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>on</sub> �<sub>, we</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e</sub> G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an re</sub>f<sub>erence c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l $y = W x + z .$ <sub>, w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $x \sim { \cal N } ( 0 , \Sigma _ { x } )$ <sub>an</sub>d $\boldsymbol { z } \sim \mathcal { N } ( \boldsymbol { 0 } , \Sigma _ { z } )$ are i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>, w</sub>ith $\Sigma _ { x } \succeq 0$ <sub>an</sub>d $\Sigma _ { z } \succ 0$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance</sub> $\Sigma _ { x }$ d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e var</sub>i<sub>ances an</sub>d <sub>corre</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>o</sub>f i<sub>npu</sub>t f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> $\Sigma _ { z }$ d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an</sub> <sub>mu</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> identit<sub>y g</sub>ives (Cover and Thomas, 2006; Telatar, 1999)

$$
\psi ( W ; \Sigma _ { x } , \Sigma _ { z } ) : = 2 I ( x ; y \mid W ) = \ln \operatorname* { d e t } \Bigl ( I _ { n } + \Sigma _ { x } ^ { 1 / 2 } W ^ { \top } \Sigma _ { z } ^ { - 1 } W \Sigma _ { x } ^ { 1 / 2 } \Bigr ) .\tag{1}
$$

W<sub>e</sub> <sub>use</sub> <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l l<sub>ogar</sub>ith<sub>ms</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>prescr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub>d <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ances;</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> A <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

NSC’s specification-only reference. NSC adopts isotropic inputs and unit noise, $\Sigma _ { x } = I _ { n }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\Sigma _ { z } = I _ { m } ,$ <sub>g</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>spec</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l<sub>-capac</sub>it<sub>y express</sub>i<sub>on</sub> $\psi ( W ; I _ { n } , I _ { m } ) = \psi ( W ) = \ln \operatorname* { d e t } ( I _ { n } + W ^ { \top } W )$ (Zhu et al., 2026). Let $\widehat { \psi } _ { \ell , j } ( p _ { b } )$ denote t<sup>h</sup>e numerica<sup>l</sup> score <sup>f</sup>or projection � in <sup>bl</sup>oc<sup>k</sup> ℓ wit<sup>h</sup> context $p _ { b }$

Architecture-level aggregation. For an architecture $a = ( ( d _ { 1 } , f _ { 1 } ) , \dots , ( d _ { L } , f _ { L } ) )$ and projection set ${ \cal M } = \{ { \sf { Q } } , { \sf { K } } , { \sf { V } } , { \sf { O } } \}$ , gate, up, down}, we define the ResNSC score by summing these numerical local

scores:

$$
S ( a ) = \sum _ { \ell = 1 } ^ { L } \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { M } } \widehat { \psi } _ { \ell , j } \big ( p _ { b ( \ell , j ) } \big ) = \sum _ { \ell = 1 } ^ { L } S _ { L } ^ { \mathrm { b l o c k } } ( p _ { 2 \ell - 2 } ; d _ { \ell } , f _ { \ell } ) .\tag{5}
$$

Representing residual history. For a fixed � with variable residual widths, two partial architectures (<sub>p</sub>refixes) at the same de<sub>p</sub>th and s<sub>p</sub>ent cost can have diferent residual led<sub>g</sub>ers and therefore diferent <sub>scores</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e same con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>ayer-an</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e use</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> NSC<sub>-</sub>DP i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> i<sub>nsu</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t for this history-dependent objective. ResNSC-DP additionally records the counts $\mathbf { n } = \left( n _ { 1 } , \ldots , n _ { K } \right)$ <sub>o</sub>f <sub>prece</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s a</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>w</sub>idth $d ^ { ( 1 ) } < \cdots < d ^ { ( K ) }$ <sub>.</sub> U<sub>n</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo-up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e re</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ger:</sub>

$$

c _ { \mathrm { f i x e d } } ( g ) - c$ <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>upper</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>score</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>any</sub> f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> ti<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>es</sub>t b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>across</sub> th<sub>e prescr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>ces</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>nes</sub> $U ( \pi )$ <sub>.</sub> I<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>s m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>mum-cos</sub>t <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> t<sub>o</sub> fi<sub>n</sub>d <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>l f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>so</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>eas</sub>ibilit<sub>y.</sub> Al<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m</sub> 1 <sub>expan</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>arges</sub>t � <sub>an</sub>d di<sub>scar</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e or</sub> d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>xes an</sub>d th<sub>ose w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>s canno</sub>t i<sub>mprove</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>score</sub> f<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>so</sub> f<sub>ar.</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> E <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>recurrence</sub> <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ure</sub> (Al<sub>g</sub>orithm 2).

Algorithm 1 ResNSC-DP: allocation with residual history.   
Input: Specifications $\mathcal { G } _ { : }$ , menus ${ \mathcal { A } } _ { g } ,$ b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $B _ { g }$ <sub>,</sub> t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance</sub> $\epsilon \geq 0$   
Output: Feasible architecture and objective bounds, or infeasibility   
Q: valid live <sub>p</sub>refixes; $\mathbf { e } _ { r ( d ) } \colon$ <sub>w</sub>idth<sub>-</sub>� <sub>coun</sub>t i<sub>ncremen</sub>t<sub>;</sub> b<sub>urgun</sub>d<sub>y: res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-</sub>hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory opera</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>   
1: Construct com<sub>p</sub>letion bounds � b<sub>y</sub> histor<sub>y</sub>-indexed backward DP (A<sub>pp</sub>endix E.3).   
2<sub>:</sub> I<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub> f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e roo</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\boldsymbol { Q }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>an</sub> i<sub>ncum</sub>b<sub>en</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>score</sub> LB<sub>; re</sub>t<sub>urn</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> if <sub>none ex</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>   
3: while $\exists \pi \in Q : U ( \pi ) > \mathrm { L B } + \epsilon$ and search limits allow do   
4: Remove ${ \boldsymbol \pi } = ( g , \mathbf { n } , c , s )$ <sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>arges</sub>t $U ( \pi )$ from Q.   
5: Reconstruct $p \gets p _ { g } ( \mathbf { n } )$ <sub>an</sub>d $t  \textstyle \sum _ { r } n _ { r } .$   
6: for each $( d , f ) \in \mathcal { A } _ { g }$ do   
7: $s ^ { \prime }  s + S _ { L _ { \sigma } } ^ { \mathrm { b l o c k } } ( \bar { p ; } d , f ) .$   
8: $\mathfrak { n ^ { \prime } }  \mathfrak { n } + \mathring { \mathbf { e } } _ { r ( d ) } , c ^ { \prime }  c + c _ { g , t + 1 } ( d , f ) ; \pi ^ { \prime }  ( g , \mathfrak { n ^ { \prime } } , c ^ { \prime } , s ^ { \prime } ) .$   
9: if no afordable completion, dominated within $( g , \mathbf { n } ^ { \prime } ) .$ , or $U ( \pi ^ { \prime } ) \leq \mathrm { L B }$ then continue.   
10: if $t + 1 = L _ { g }$ then update the incumbent and LB only on improvement.   
11: else insert $\overline { { \boldsymbol { \pi } } } ^ { \prime }$ i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> $\boldsymbol { Q }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e cos</sub>t<sub>–score</sub> f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er a</sub>t $( g , \mathbf { n } ^ { \prime } )$   
12: end for   
13: end while   
14: return incumbent architecture and (LB, UB), where $\operatorname { U B } = \operatorname* { m a x } \ ( \{ \operatorname { L B } \} \cup \{ U ( \pi ) : \pi \in Q \} )$

Optimality guarantees. Let $S ^ { * }$ be the o<sub>p</sub>timum of the numerical objective in E<sub>q</sub>uation (6). Results. Reverse achieves 59.49% Avg8 versus 68.87% for ResNSC<sub>,</sub> a 9.38-<sub>p</sub>oint diference at the same <sub>p</sub>arameter count and NSC score (Table 4). Th<sub>e p</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d FFN <sub>w</sub>idth<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> <sub>a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht i<sub>n</sub>h<sub>er</sub>it<sub>ance.</sub>

## 4.3. Ranking across architecture search spaces

Setup. We examine whether incorporating residual context retains NSC’s global ranking utility while distin<sub>g</sub>uishin<sub>g</sub> more candidate architectures. Followin<sub>g</sub> the NSC <sub>p</sub>rotocol (Zhu et al., 2026), we com<sub>p</sub>are the scores on 500 FlexiBERT architectures (Serianni and Kalita, 2023), 200 GPT-2 architectures (Radford et al., 2019), and 1,001 AutoFormer-Small candidates (Chen et al., 2021), <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es,</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>-per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 5 <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>s ran</sub>k <sub>corre</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>t<sub>-score coun</sub>t<sub>s;</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> I <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>supp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>ary resu</sub>lt<sub>s.</sub>

## 5. Conclusion

We introduced Residual-Aware Neural S<sub>p</sub>ectral Ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub> (ResNSC), which scores each Transformer projection in the residual context implied by its architecture, using a reference constructed from th<sub>e arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure spec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>l<sub>one.</sub> B<sub>ecause ear</sub>li<sub>er w</sub>idth <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ces c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>er scores,</sub> R<sub>es</sub>NSC<sub>-</sub>DP <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> NSC<sub>-</sub>DP <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-</sub>hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>an</sub>d b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>score</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e</sub> <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e se</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

## A.1. Gaussian identity, including singular sources

Fix $W \in \mathbb { R } ^ { m \times n } , \Sigma _ { x } \succeq 0$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $\Sigma _ { z } \succ 0$ . Let

$$
x \sim N ( 0 , \Sigma _ { x } ) , \qquad z \sim N ( 0 , \Sigma _ { z } ) , \qquad x \perp z , \qquad y = W x + z .\tag{A.1}
$$

The out<sub>p</sub>ut covariances are $\operatorname { C o v } ( y \mid W ) = W \Sigma _ { x } W ^ { \top } + \Sigma _ { z }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\operatorname { C o v } ( y \mid x , W ) = \Sigma _ { z }$ <sub>.</sub> S<sub>u</sub>bt<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> Gaussian diferential entro<sub>p</sub>ies <sub>g</sub>ives (Cover and Thomas, 2006; Telatar, 1999)

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { 2 I ( x ; y \mid W ) = \ln \operatorname* { d e t } ( W \Sigma _ { x } W ^ { \top } + \Sigma _ { z } ) - \ln \operatorname* { d e t } ( \Sigma _ { z } ) } \\ & { \phantom { 2 a b s } = \ln \operatorname* { d e t } ( I _ { m } + \Sigma _ { z } ^ { - 1 / 2 } W \Sigma _ { x } W ^ { \top } \Sigma _ { z } ^ { - 1 / 2 } ) } \\ & { \phantom { 2 a b s } = \ln \operatorname* { d e t } ( I _ { n } + \Sigma _ { x } ^ { 1 / 2 } W ^ { \top } \Sigma _ { z } ^ { - 1 } W \Sigma _ { x } ^ { 1 / 2 } ) . } \end{array}\tag{A.2}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>as</sub>t <sub>equa</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> S<sub>y</sub>l<sub>ves</sub>t<sub>er</sub>’<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>nan</sub>t id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y.</sub> Th<sub>e argumen</sub>t <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns va</sub>lid f<sub>or s</sub>i<sub>ngu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> $\Sigma _ { x }$ b<sub>ecause</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ances are pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e quan</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>w</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> th<sub>e mu</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>prescr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub>d G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an</sub> <sub>source.</sub>

For a random reference projection, we average this pointwise expression over its ensemble. For a write projection, the source covariance is itself generated by $W _ { b } ^ { \mathrm { i n } }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en app</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>on</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> t<sub>a</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r expec</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## B. The residual reference and its induced metrics

## B.1. Residual windows and a process realizing the ledger

Let $A _ { u } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { u \times M }$ <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>� res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es.</sub> A b<sub>ranc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>w</sub>idth $\widetilde { d } _ { b }$ h<sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l f<sub>orm</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { h _ { b + 1 } = h _ { b } + A _ { \widetilde { d } _ { b } } ^ { \top } F _ { b } \Big ( \mathrm { N o r m } ( A _ { \widetilde { d } _ { b } } h _ { b } ) \Big ) . } \end{array}\tag{B.1}
$$

Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>nser</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>preserves</sub> <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>wr</sub>it<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow,</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> used in VWT (Wu et al., 2026). Both branches of block ℓ read and write its first $d _ { \ell }$ <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es.</sub>

T<sub>o cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t <sub>an arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure-on</sub>l<sub>y re</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce an aux</sub>ili<sub>ary process</sub> $\widetilde { h _ { b } }$ :

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { \widetilde { h } _ { 0 } \sim N \big ( 0 , \mathrm { d i a g } _ { i \le M } { \mathbf 1 } \{ i \le E \} \big ) , } & \\ & { \widetilde { h } _ { b + 1 } = \widetilde { h } _ { b } + A _ { \widetilde { d } _ { b } } ^ { \top } \varepsilon _ { b } , } & { \varepsilon _ { b } \sim N ( 0 , I _ { \widetilde { d } _ { b } } ) . } \end{array}\tag{B.2}
$$

Joint observation of a shared source. Let $x \sim { \cal N } ( 0 , \Sigma _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e a d } } )$ <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t h<sub>ea</sub>d ℎ <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub> $y _ { h } = W _ { h } x +$ ${ z } _ { h . }$ <sub>, w</sub>ith <sub>mu</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>un</sub>it G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an rece</sub>i<sub>ver no</sub>i<sub>ses,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>�.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> $K _ { h } \ =$ $( \Sigma _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e a d } } ) ^ { 1 / 2 } W _ { h } ^ { \top } W _ { h } ( \bar { \Sigma } _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e a d } } ) ^ { 1 / \hat { 2 } } \succeq 0$ <sub>.</sub> St<sub>ac</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub>

$$

Proposition 1 (Residual-history suficiency and dominance). $F i x g$ and the two-write reference. Prefixes with the same count vector n have identical additional scores for every common sufix. If their remaining budgets agree, their feasible continuation sets agree. If their spent costs and accumulated scores satisfy $c _ { 1 } \leq c _ { 2 }$ and $s _ { 1 } \geq s _ { 2 } ,$ , respectively, then the first prefix dominates the second.

## F. Training and evaluation statistics

Data and training budget. Pretraining uses FineWeb-Edu (Penedo et al., 2024), a 32,000-token Ll<sub>a</sub>m<sub>a</sub>-2 t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>e</sub>niz<sub>e</sub>r<sub>, a</sub>nd <sub>pac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>seque</sub>n<sub>ces o</sub>f l<sub>e</sub>n<sub>g</sub>th 4<sub>,</sub>096<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> fix<sub>e</sub>d <sub>co</sub>r<sub>pus su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>s co</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>in <sub>app</sub>r<sub>o</sub>ximatel<sub>y</sub> 8.34B available tokens at 121M and 37.45B at 374M. The smaller subset uses the first ten of th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>arger</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> 45 <sub>source</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit b<sub>y</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> 99<sub>.</sub>5% t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d 0<sub>.</sub>5% <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> All <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures a</sub>t <sub>a g</sub>i<sub>ven sca</sub>l<sub>e use</sub> th<sub>e same su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>, sp</sub>lit<sub>, an</sub>d t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

The <sub>g</sub>lobal batch contains 128 se<sub>q</sub>uences<sub>,</sub> or 524<sub>,</sub>288 tokens. We set the main-<sub>p</sub>hase bud<sub>g</sub>et to <sub>approx</sub>i<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> 20 t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens per parame</sub>t<sub>er us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>if<sub>orm mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er coun</sub>t<sub>,</sub> followin<sub>g</sub> the bud<sub>g</sub>et convention motivated b<sub>y</sub> com<sub>p</sub>ute-o<sub>p</sub>timal trainin<sub>g</sub> (Hofmann et al., 2022). The main <sub>p</sub>hase contains 4<sub>,</sub>616 u<sub>p</sub>dates at 121M and 14<sub>,</sub>267 at 374M<sub>,</sub> corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> to a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> 2.42B and 7.48B tokens. Evaluation protocol. FineWeb-Edu cross-entropy is measured on the same 2,048 held-out sequences across arc<sup>hi</sup>tectures an<sup>d</sup> runs, eac<sup>h</sup> contr<sup>ib</sup>ut<sup>i</sup>ng 4,09<sup>6</sup> next-to<sup>k</sup>en targets. We use lm-eval 0.4.13 (Gao et al., 2023) with BF16 models, the shared Llama-2 tokenizer, a maximum in<sub>p</sub>ut len<sub>g</sub>th of 4,096, and zero-shot task tem<sub>p</sub>lates. Each task uses its com<sub>p</sub>lete evaluation s<sub>p</sub>lit (Table 12).

At both scales<sub>,</sub> we re<sub>p</sub>ort WikiText-2 word <sub>p</sub>er<sub>p</sub>lexit<sub>y</sub> and LAMBADA final-word <sub>p</sub>er<sub>p</sub>lexit<sub>y</sub>.

## F.1. Scoring ablation protocol

E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 3 <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s an arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> 121M <sub>w</sub>idth <sub>menus un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e same parame</sub>t<sub>er ce</sub>ili<sub>ng,</sub> th<sub>en uses</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>ove.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t th<sub>ree</sub> simplifications of residual history. Without residual context uses isotropic normalized source powers and identity read and receiver metrics, while retaining within-branch propagation. Mean history only uses the average reference power over the <sub>curren</sub>t <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es w</sub>h<sub>en eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ng eac</sub>h b<sub>ranc</sub>h<sub>, re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng mean power w</sub>hil<sub>e remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> dif<sub>erences.</sub>

To test propagated features, without propagated spectrum replaces the write-source covariance $\Sigma _ { x , b } ^ { \mathrm { w r i t e } }$ with the appropriately sized identity for both attention output and FFN down projections, <sub>preserv</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> <sub>power</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>propaga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>spec</sub>t<sub>rum.</sub> R<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>ver</sub> <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>unc</sub>h<sub>ange</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

## G. Layer-wise architecture configurations

Architecture and allocation rules. Each Transformer block (Vaswani et al., 2017) contains pre-RMSNorm (Zhan<sub>g</sub> and Sennrich, 2019) causal self-attention followed b<sub>y</sub> a <sub>p</sub>re-RMSNorm SwiGLU FFN (Shazeer, 2020). Attention uses rotar<sub>y p</sub>osition embeddin<sub>g</sub>s (Su et al., 2021) and 16 <sub>q</sub>uer<sub>y</sub> and 16 k<sub>ey</sub>/<sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>s, w</sub>ith h<sub>ea</sub>d di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on</sub> $d _ { \ell } / 1 6$ . The FFN has gate and up projections from $d _ { \ell }$ to $f _ { \ell }$ and a down projection back to $d _ { \ell }$ . Linear projections are bias-free, and input embeddings and <sub>voca</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ary rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>are un</sub>ti<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> I<sub>nac</sub>ti<sub>ve res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r va</sub>l<sub>ues a</sub>l<sub>ong</sub> th<sub>e</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers.</sub> Th<sub>e em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub>/<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>w</sub>idth i<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t $E = 6 4 0$ for 121M and $E = 1 0 2 4$ for 374M. Final RMSNorm acts on the last active width $d _ { L } ,$ <sub>,</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s res</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d <sub>or</sub> <sub>croppe</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> <sub>rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>w</sub>idth �<sub>.</sub>

We compare six allocation rules. Uniform uses $d _ { \ell } = E$ <sub>an</sub>d $f _ { \ell } = 2 . 7 5 E$ . VWT uses the default bottleneck-location and width-ratio settings (0.75, 0.3), with $f _ { \ell } = 4 d _ { \ell }$ . TLM holds residual width fixed and a<sub>pp</sub>lies a cosine FFN ta<sub>p</sub>er from 1.5 to 0.5 times the uniform FFN width (Ba<sub>y</sub>at et al., 2026). ResNSC jointly selects active residual and FFN widths. NSC maximizes the original NSC score over the same residual-width and FFN-width menu under the same parameter ceiling. Reverse reverses the ResNSC se<sub>q</sub>uence of $( d _ { \ell } , f _ { \ell } )$ pairs, preserving the projection-shape inventory used by the score.

Search menus. At each scale, the active-width menu is $d _ { \ell } / E \in \{ 0 . 2 5 , 0 . 5 0 , \ldots , 3 . 0 0 \}$ . Architecture and variance conventions. FlexiBERT reads widths from each architecture configuration and retains the archived inventory: merged QKV for self-attention, two square matrices f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> LT <sub>opera</sub>t<sub>or, one square ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x</sub> f<sub>or</sub> DSC<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e arc</sub>hi<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d<sub>-ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x</sub> d<sub>ecompos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub> FFN<sub>s.</sub> Thi<sub>s mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>FFN i<sub>nven</sub>t<sub>ory</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> th<sub>e source</sub> <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> FFN<sub>s sequen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y.</sub> GPT<sub>-</sub>2 <sub>uses</sub> th<sub>e arc</sub>hi<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ry s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d d<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>02<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>nven</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>es re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> NSC <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ape-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence var</sub>i<sub>ances.</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>F<sub>ormer uses</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d dimension 64 and internal attention width 64�, including a rectangular output projection. Both scores use the same architectural specifications; their Q/K/V evaluation conventions are distinguished b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow.</sub>

Residual and FFN adaptation. Each candidate has constant residual width across its layers, giving <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d b<sub>ranc</sub>h<sub>-</sub>i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance</sub> $\Sigma _ { x , b } ^ { \mathrm { i n } } = I$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>rea</sub>d/<sub>wr</sub>it<sub>e me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> i<sub>s app</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d <sub>across</sub> th<sub>ese arc</sub>hi<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r pos</sub>t<sub>-norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s.</sub> Ordinary FFNs have one up/read projection and one propagated down/write projection, with Gaussian hidd<sub>en re</sub>f<sub>erence an</sub>d <sub>un</sub>it <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e propaga</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>uses</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an c</sub>l<sub>osure</sub> i<sub>n p</sub>l<sub>ace o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> GELU covariance kernel. For GPT-2 and AutoFormer<sub>,</sub> NSC retains its headwise decom<sub>p</sub>osition<sub>,</sub> while ResNSC evaluates complete Q/K/V projections with the inherited per-head entry variance and uses a <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>propaga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue-</sub>t<sub>o-ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

Ranking within a 10% parameter window.
